\mathbf{B}_{1}
into
\mathbf{B}_{2}
do not affect
\mathcal{P}_{y,1}
.

We now look at the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graphs
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
. Item (iii) combined with
B_{x}=B_{x^{\prime},i}^{\prime}
implies that a fold of type IIA based on
f^{\prime}\in EB^{\prime}
can be applied to
\mathcal{B}_{i}^{\prime}
. We denote this fold by
F_{i}^{\prime}
. Moreover, since all edges in
Star(x^{\prime},B^{\prime})\setminus\{f^{\prime}\}
have the same label in
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}
and in
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime}
it follows that
f^{\prime}
can be folded with some edge in
Star(x,B_{1}^{\prime})\cap E(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime})
if, and only if,
f^{\prime}
can be folded with some edge in
Star(x,B_{2}^{\prime})\cap E(\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime})
.

Sub-case a.

f^{\prime}
cannot be folded with an edge in
Star(x,B_{1}^{\prime})\cap E(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime})
. Since
x
is peripheral it follows that
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are tame.

Let
\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\prime\prime}
be the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\mathbf{B}_{i}^{\prime}
by the fold
F_{i}^{\prime}
. Therefore the partitioned tuple at
y^{\prime}=\omega(f^{\prime})
in
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
is equal to

\mathcal{P}_{y^{\prime},1}^{\prime\prime}=(T_{y}\oplus(b_{1}^{-1}b_{2}),(\gamma)\oplus P_{y})

and in
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
is equal to

\mathcal{P}_{y^{\prime},2}^{\prime\prime}:=(T_{y}\oplus(b_{1}^{-1}\omega_{e}(c^{-1}d)b_{2},(\gamma)\oplus P_{y})

where
\gamma:=b_{1}^{-1}\omega_{e}(a_{e}^{z})b_{1}
and
z\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\langle a_{e}^{z}\rangle=\alpha_{e}^{-1}(a^{-1}B_{x}a)=\alpha_{e}^{-1}(B_{x}).
It follows from condition (4) of marked
\mathbb{A}
-graphs that
b_{1}^{-1}\omega_{e}(c^{-1}d)b_{1}\in\langle\gamma\rangle
which implies that

\mathcal{P}_{y^{\prime},1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{P}_{y^{\prime},2}^{\prime\prime}

are equivalent.
[
Dut
, Lemma 4.17]
implies that
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
are equivalent. Therefore
F_{1}^{\prime}
is bad if, and only if,
F_{2}^{\prime}
is bad. This shows that (A) occurs if
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are bad and that (C) occurs if
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are good.

Sub-case b.
there is
h^{\prime}\in Star(x^{\prime},B^{\prime})\cap E
that can be folded with
f^{\prime}
. An argument similar to that of sub-case (a) shows
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
is equivalent to
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
if
\omega(h^{\prime})=y^{\prime}
. Thus
\omega(h^{\prime})
and
y
must be distinct.

Let
F_{i}^{\prime\prime}
denote the fold that identifies
f^{\prime}
and
h^{\prime}
and let
u^{\prime\prime}
denote the common image of the vertices
y^{\prime}
and
\omega(h^{\prime})
. As the group of
f^{\prime}
is trivial,
F_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime\prime}
are tame. Moreover, by Lemma
4.30
, we may assume that
F_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime\prime}
are elementary since the group of
B_{h^{\prime},1}^{\prime}\neq 1\neq B_{h^{\prime},2}^{\prime}
.

If
\omega(h^{\prime})
is of orbifold type, then
F_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime\prime}
are bad since
B_{y^{\prime},1}^{\prime}\neq 1\neq B_{y^{\prime},2}^{\prime}
. Thus assume that
\omega(h^{\prime})
is exceptional. Observe that we may without loos of generality assume that

(T,P):=\mathcal{P}_{\omega(h^{\prime}),1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{\omega(h^{\prime}),2}^{\prime}.

Hence the partitioned tuple at
u^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
is equal to

\mathcal{P}_{u^{\prime\prime},1}^{\prime\prime}=(T\oplus T_{y}\oplus(b_{1}^{-1}b_{2}),(\gamma)\oplus P\oplus P_{y})

while the partitioned tuple at
u
in
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
is equal to

\mathcal{P}_{u^{\prime\prime},2}^{\prime\prime}=(T\oplus T_{y}\oplus(b_{1}^{-1}\omega_{e}(c^{-1}d)b_{2}),(\gamma)\oplus P\oplus P_{y}).

It is not hard to see that these partitioned tuples are equivalent which implies that
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
is equivalent to
\mathcal{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
. This shows that
F_{1}^{\prime\prime}
is good if, and only if,
F_{2}^{\prime\prime}
is good.

Case 2.

x=\alpha(f_{1})=\alpha(f_{2})
is non-peripheral. In this case the group of
y=\omega(f_{1})=\omega(f_{2})
in
\mathcal{B}_{i}
(
i=1,2
) is trivial since
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are good. In particular, the group of edges staring at
y
is also trivial.

By definition,
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
replace the trivial group
B_{y}=B_{y,1}=B_{y,2}
by the non-trivial groups

B_{y,1}^{\prime}=\langle b_{1}^{-1}b_{2}\rangle
and
B_{y,2}^{\prime}=\langle b_{1}^{-1}\omega_{e}(c^{-1}d)b_{2}\rangle

respectively. Thus a fold of type IIA based on
(f^{\prime})^{-1}
can be applied to
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
and to
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
. On the other hand, (iii) implies that

B_{x^{\prime},1}^{\prime}\cap a\alpha_{e}(A_{e})a^{-1}\neq 1\ \ \text{ and }\ \ B_{x^{\prime},2}^{\prime}\cap\alpha_{1}a\alpha_{e}(c^{-1})\alpha_{e}(A_{e})\alpha_{e}(c)a^{-1}\alpha_{1}^{-1}\neq 1.

Lemma
3.40
therefore implies that
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are bad.

(D2)
It follows from Corollary
4.32
that
\mathbf{B}_{1}
can be replaced by an equivalent marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
such that the following hold:

(i)

the labels of
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
do not change and the labels of
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
are of type
(c,\bar{e},d_{1})
and
(c,\bar{e},d_{2})
, see Figure
34
.

(ii)

the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}^{\prime}
that is obtained from
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
by the fold
\bar{F}_{1}
that identifies
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
is equivalent to
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
.

(iii)

if
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
denotes the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
by an A0 move that makes the labels of
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
coincide, then the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
by the fold
\bar{F}_{2}
that identifies
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
is equivalent to
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
.

Figure 34:
The marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph
\bar{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
in the three configurations that can occur).

Therefore we can assume that
\mathbf{B}_{1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
(resp.
\mathbf{B}_{2}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
) and
\bar{F}_{1}=F_{1}
(resp.
F_{2}=\bar{F}_{2}
).

We first consider the case that
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
are peripheral. As
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are good, at most one one of them has non-trivial vertex group. Thus there are two cases: If
B_{y_{1},1}=B_{y_{2},1}=1
, then we are in case (B) since the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
by folding the edges
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
is equivalent to the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
by folding the edges
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
. If either
B_{y_{1},1}\neq 1
or
B_{y_{2},1}\neq 1
, then we are in case (A). Indeed, the fold that identifies the edges
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
(resp.
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
) in
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
(resp. in
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
) is bad.

We now consider the case that
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
are non-peripheral. If one of the vertices
y_{1}
or
y_{2}
is of orbifold type, then the fact that
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are good implies that the other vertex has trivial group. In this case the same argument as in the previous paragraph shows that (A) holds.

Finally assume that
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
are exceptional vertices, see Figure
35
. Note that the graph does not always look exactly as in the figure, as the other two configurations of Figure
34
might also occur. However, in these situations exactly the same arguments can be applied.

Figure 35:

{F}_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
produce equivalent marked
\mathbb{A}
-graphs.

The loop
g_{1}\cup g_{2}
lies in the core of the
\mathbb{A}
-graph
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}
underlying
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
. As
F_{2}
is tame, the group of at least one of the edges
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
is trivial in
\mathcal{B}_{1}
, and therefore also in
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}^{\prime}
. Putting these facts together we conclude that
core(\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime})
cannot be a special almost orbifold covering with a good marking. Therefore, as
F_{1}
is good, the partitioned tuple

\mathcal{P}_{y,1}^{\prime}:=(T_{y_{1},1}\oplus T_{y_{2},1},P_{y_{1},1}\oplus P_{y_{2},1})

is non-critical of simple type, where
y
denotes the common image of
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
in
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}
. The same argument shows that the partitioned tuple

\mathcal{P}_{y,2}^{\prime}:=(T_{y_{1},2}\oplus T_{y_{2},2},P_{y_{1},2}\oplus P_{y_{2},2})=(T_{y_{1},1}\oplus T_{y_{2},1}^{d_{2}^{-1}d_{1}},P_{y_{1},1}\oplus P_{y_{2},1}^{d_{2}^{-1}d_{1}})

is of simple type.

Let
F_{1}^{\prime}
denote the tame elementary fold of type IIIA that identifies the edges
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
in
{\mathbf{B}}_{1}^{\prime}
and let
{\mathbf{B}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
denote the resulting marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph. Similarly, let
F_{2}^{\prime}
denote the tame elementary fold of type IIIA that identifies the edges
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
in
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
and let
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
denote the resulting marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph, see Figure
35
. Note that
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
only differ at the vertex
y:=\omega(f)=\omega(g)
where the partitioned tuples are given by

\mathcal{P}_{y,1}^{\prime\prime}:=(T_{y_{1},1}\oplus T_{y_{2},1}\oplus(d_{1}^{-1}d_{2}),P_{y_{1},1}\oplus P_{y_{2},1})

and

\mathcal{P}_{y,2}^{\prime\prime}:=(T_{y_{1},1}\oplus T_{y_{2},1}^{d_{1}^{-1}d_{2}}\oplus(d_{1}^{-1}d_{2}),P_{y_{1},1}\oplus P_{y_{2},1}^{d_{2}^{-1}d_{1}}),

respectively. One easily verifies that
\mathcal{P}_{y,1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathcal{P}_{y,2}^{\prime\prime}
are equivalent. Therefore, by
[
Dut
, Lemma 3.16]
,
{\mathbf{B}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
are equivalent.

The equivalence between
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime\prime}
implies that the fold
F_{1}^{\prime}
is good if, and only if, the fold
F_{2}^{\prime}
is good. Therefore we are in case (A) if
F_{1}^{\prime}
is good and we are in case (B) if
F_{1}^{\prime}
is bad.

(D3)

F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are folds of type IIIA with
f_{1}=g_{1}^{-1}
and
f_{2}=g_{2}^{-1}
. We may assume without loss of generality that
x=\alpha(f_{1})=\alpha(f_{2})
is peripheral (and hence
w=\omega(f_{1})=\omega(f_{2})
is non-peripheral).

It follows from Corollary
4.32
that
\mathbf{B}_{1}
can be replaced by an equivalent marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
such that the following hold:

(i)

the label of
f_{1}
is
(a,e,b)
and the label of
f_{2}
is
(a,e,\omega_{e}(c)b)
for some non-trivial element
c\in A_{e}
.

(ii)

the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
by the fold
\bar{F}_{1}
that identifies
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
is equivalent to
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
.

(iii)

if
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
denotes the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
by an A1 move that makes the label of
f_{2}
equal to
(a\alpha_{e}(c),e,b)
, then the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
by the fold
\bar{F}_{2}
that identifies
f_{1}^{-1}
and
g_{2}^{-1}
is equivalent to
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
.

Therefore we can assume that
\mathbf{B}_{1}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{1}
(resp.
\mathbf{B}_{2}=\bar{\mathbf{B}}_{2}
) and that
F_{1}=\bar{F}_{1}
(resp.
F_{2}=\bar{F}_{2}
), see Fig.
36
.
Denote
\gamma:=b^{-1}\omega_{e}(c)b\in A_{[w]}
and
\gamma^{\prime}:=a^{-1}\alpha_{e}(c)a=\alpha_{e}(c)\in A_{[x]}.

Figure 36:
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are of type IIIA and
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are of type IIA.

Since
F_{1}
is good,
w
is exceptional and

\mathcal{P}_{w,1}=(T_{w,1},P_{w,1})
is non-critical of simple type. On the other hand, since
F_{2}
is good,
B_{x}=1
. Thus
B_{g}=1
for all
g\in Star(x,B)
. In particular,
B_{f_{1}}=B_{f_{2}}=1
.

We first look at
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
. It follows from Proposition
3.43
that
(T_{w,1}\oplus(\gamma),P_{w,1})
cannot be of almost orbifold covering type. Thus, as
F_{1}
is good,

\mathcal{P}_{w,1}^{\prime}=(T_{w,1}\oplus(\gamma),P_{w,1})

is non-critical of simple type. Observe that the group of
(f^{\prime})^{-1}
is trivial (in
\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime}
) and
(f^{\prime})^{-1}
violates condition (F2) of folded
\mathbb{A}
-graphs since the peripheral element
\gamma
lies in
B_{w,1}^{\prime}
.

Let
F_{1}^{\prime}
denote the type IIA fold based on
(f^{\prime})^{-1}
. As
\gamma
is part of a minimal generating tuple of
B_{y,1}^{\prime}
it follows that

b^{-1}\omega_{e}(A_{e})b\cap B_{y,1}^{\prime}=\langle\gamma\rangle
and that
(f^{\prime})^{-1}
cannot be folded with any edge in
Star(w,B_{1}^{\prime})\cap E(\mathcal{B}_{1}^{\prime})
. Thus
F_{1}^{\prime}
is tame elementary.

We now look at
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
. Let
F_{2}^{\prime}
be the type IIA fold based on the edge
f^{\prime}
. Note that
B_{h,2}^{\prime}=1
for all
h\in Star(w,B_{2}^{\prime})
since
B_{h}=1
for all
h\in Star(w,B)
. Thus
F_{2}^{\prime}
is tame elementary.

It is not hard to see that the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
by the fold
F_{1}^{\prime}
coincides with the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
by the fold
F_{2}^{\prime}
. Hence
F_{1}^{\prime}
is good if, and only if,
F_{2}^{\prime}
is good. Therefore (A) holds if
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are bad and (B) occurs otherwise.

Remark 4.44
.

Observe that
F_{2}^{\prime}
(and hence
F_{1}^{\prime}
) is good exactly when
(T_{w,1},(\gamma)\oplus P_{w,1})
is non-critical and of simple type. This follows from the fact that the resulting marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph cannot be a special almost orbifold cover with a good marking as the vertex
x
becomes a boundary vertex of a degenerate sub-
\mathbb{A}
-graph of orbifold type such that there is only one edge incident at
x
that has non trivial group, namely the edge
f
.

(D4)
As
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are good it follows that
B_{y}=B_{z}=1
where
y=\omega(f)
and
z=\omega(g)
. This case is illustrated in Figure
37
in the case that
y=z
.

Let
F_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.
F_{2}^{\prime}
) be the fold of type IIA based on
g
(resp.
f)
of
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
). Observe that both folds are tame because the corresponding peripheral elements
\gamma_{f}^{\prime}
and
\gamma_{g}^{\prime}
are part of a minimal generating set of

B_{x,1}^{\prime}=B_{x,2}^{\prime}=B_{x,1}=B_{x,2}\leq A_{[x]}.
Moreover, both are elementary because they satisfy condition (El.2). It is not hard to see that
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are good (resp. bad) if and only if
y\neq z
(resp.
y=z
), see Fig.
33
.

Figure 37:
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are bad tame elementary folds of type IIA.

(D5)

\mathbf{B}:=\mathbf{B}_{1}=\mathbf{B}_{2}
and
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are of type IIA with
f\neq g
such that
f
and
g
can be folded in
\mathcal{B}:=\mathcal{B}_{1}=\mathcal{B}_{2}
by an elementary fold. Note that
F_{i}
is not tame after
F_{j}
is applied. Let
F_{i}^{\prime}
denote the tame elementary fold that identifies
f
and
g
in
\mathcal{B}_{i}^{\prime}
.
Since
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are good,
\omega(f)
and
\omega(g)
are either both peripheral with trivial group or both exceptional. We will give a complete argument for the case they are exceptional and distinct so that
F_{i}^{\prime}
are of type IA.

The fact that
f
and
g
have the same label implies that the peripheral element
\gamma_{f^{-1}}^{\prime}
added by
F_{1}
to the partitioned tuple
\mathcal{P}_{\omega(f)}
coincides with the peripheral element
\gamma_{g^{-1}}^{\prime}
added by
F_{2}
to the partitioned tuple
\mathcal{P}_{\omega(g)}
. Therefore the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
by
F_{1}^{\prime}
coincides with the marked
\mathbb{A}
-graph that is obtained from
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
by
F_{2}^{\prime}
. This implies that
F_{1}^{\prime}
is good if and only if
F_{2}^{\prime}
is good. The hole argument is illustrated in Figure
38
.

Figure 38:
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma_{f^{-1}}^{\prime}=\gamma_{g^{-1}}^{\prime}
.

Generic case.
Assume that the conclusions of Lemmas
4.35
and
4.37
hold.
Since
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are good, the argument used in the generic case when one of the folds is bad shows that a fold
F_{2}^{\prime}
(resp.
F_{1}^{\prime}
) that is essentially the same as
F_{2}
(resp.
F_{1}
) can be applied to
\mathbf{B}_{1}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathbf{B}_{2}^{\prime}
) and
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
yield the same
\mathbb{A}
-graph
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
, i.e.
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
commute.

Therefore to complete the proof it suffices to show that
F_{1}^{\prime}
is good if and only if
F_{2}^{\prime}
is good. This follows by inspecting the various cases. We will give a sample argument for the case
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are of type IIA, and so
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
are also of type IIA. If
\omega(f)\neq\omega(g)
then claim is trivial. Thus assume that
y=\omega(f)=\omega(g)=z
. As
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are good,
y
is either exceptional or peripheral with trivial group. If
x
is exceptional then the claim is also trivial. If
x
is peripheral, then
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
turn
x
into a vertex of orbifold type. Consequently
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
do not induce markings on
\mathcal{B}^{\prime\prime}
and therefore are bad.

5
Horizontal Heegaard splittings

Throughout this section we assume familiarity with horizontal Heegaard splittings of Seifert manifolds as introduced by Moriah and Schultens
[
MS
]
.

Let
M
be an orientable Seifert 3-manifold over the base orbifold
\mathcal{O}
, let
\pi:M\to\mathcal{O}
be the canoncial map. Given a horizontal Heegaard splitting we obtain the following:

1.

a surface
\Sigma\subset M
with a single boundary component, the regular neighborhood of this surface can be thought of as one of the handlebodies of the splitting.

2.

An open disk
D\subset\mathcal{O}
containing at most one cone point such that
\pi(\Sigma)=\mathcal{O}\setminus D
and the map
\pi|_{\Sigma}:\Sigma\to\mathcal{O}\setminus D
is an orbifold cover.

It follows in particular that the map
\pi|_{\Sigma}:\Sigma\to\mathcal{O}
is an almost orbifold cover. Note that this is an amost orbifold cover of a very special type as
\pi^{-1}(D)
contains no disks but consists of a single 1-sphere.

Theorem 5.1

(Theorem
1.11
)
.

Let
M
be a non-small orientable Seifert 3-manifold with orientable base space and
T
be a tuple corresponding to a horizontal Heegaard splitting. Then
T
is irreducible iff the Heegaard splitting is irreducible.

Proof.

As reducible Heegard splitting always define reducible generating tuples it suffices to show that the generating tuples corresponding to irreducible Heegaard splitting are irreducible. Thus we may assume that the horizontal Heegaard splitting under consideration is irreducible.

Let
\Sigma
and
D
be as above and let
T:=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})
a basis of the free group
\pi_{1}(\Sigma)
, thus

i_{*}(T)=(i_{*}(x_{1}),\ldots,i_{*}(x_{n}))

is a generating set of
\pi_{1}(M)
if
i:\Sigma\to M
is the inclusion map. We need to show that
i_{*}(T)
is irreducible. To do so it suffices to show that

\pi_{*}\circ i_{*}(T)=(\pi|_{\Sigma})_{*}(T)

is irreducible.

This claim follows from Theorem
1.9
once we have verified that the almost orbifold covering
\pi|_{\Sigma}:\Sigma\to\mathcal{O}
is special and that
T
is rigid.

The rigidity of
T
is trivial as a simple homology argument shows that the element of surfact group corresponding to the single boundary component of a surface is never part of a basis.

Let now
m=1
if
D
contains no cone point, otherwise let
m
be the order of the single cone point contained in
D
. Let
m_{1},\ldots,m_{k}
be the orders of the cone points in
\Sigma\setminus D
and
d=lcm(m_{1},\ldots,m_{k})
. It follows from the construction of horizontal Heegaard splittings that
d
is the degree of the almost orbifold covering
\pi|_{\Sigma}
, see chapter 4 of
[
Se
]
. The irreducibility of the Heegaard splitting morever implies that
m>d
unless
\mathcal{O}
is a surface, a case when the claim is trivial. This follows from
[
Se
]
.

To see that
\pi|_{\Sigma}
is special it remains to show that
d
does not divide
m
. If
d
divides
m
the
\pi|_{\Sigma}
factors through a finite sheeted cover of
\mathcal{O}
which contradicts the assumtion that
(\pi|_{\Sigma})_{*}(T)
is a generating tuple of
\pi_{1}(\mathcal{O})
. Thus
d
divides
m
and the Theorem is proven.
∎

Note that the proof actually implies that Heegaard splittings are irreducible. Thus we reprove Theorem 8.1 of
[
Se
]
in the case of non-small Seifert manifolds.
